| `Roth.RSet` | `Roth.wronskianMatrix` | no | A: The admissible family consists of nonzero integer polynomials with coordinate degree bounds and absolute coefficient bound. Mechanism: Intersect the nonzero condition with pointwise degree and coefficient inequalities. B: Defines the square matrix whose rows are successive univariate Hasse derivatives of a finite polynomial family. Mechanism: Matrix.of bundles the function sending row and column indices to Polynomial.hasseDeriv of that column polynomial at the row order. Wronskian constructs determinants and detects independent univariate families. Theta uses a nonzero separated derivative determinant to compare index upper/lower bounds, then solves scalar inequalities and performs dimension induction.
